# Phi implementation ledger

## CURRENT — OmniRoute Pi RPC import fixed; host endpoint/egress still blocked (2026-10-29)

Starting branch/HEAD: `feature/phi-optional-services-startup` / `161ac914e0bcc4a9da7a0790a1fbb6835942ed29`. Preserved the existing dirty Caddy candidate fix/tests/docs and untracked `logs/`. This unit changed only OmniRoute extension adapter resolution and focused tests; it did not touch SearXNG, Second Brain, stable Phi, Docker objects, credentials, or provider policy.

Installed Pi 0.87.1's `@earendil-works/pi-ai` declares the OpenAI-completions adapter under its import-only `./api/*` package export. A worktree-loaded extension cannot resolve that peer package from its source directory. The extension now resolves the declared export target within the detected Pi runtime and reuses its `streamSimple`; no new stream implementation was added. Actual Pi RPC regression PASS: the worktree extension loaded, registered/listed eight OmniRoute models using a fixture-only key, accepted `set_model` for `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-high`, and returned the selected provider/model in `get_state`. No prompt, model event, inference, or provider call occurred. Offline TLS/auth/origin/replacement/SSE/tool/cancellation and candidate-Caddy regressions: **11/11 PASS**. `node scripts/typecheck.mjs`, `node --check lib/omniroute-tls.mjs`, and `git diff --check` PASS.

Reuse operator evidence without repeating it: Caddy at the Docker-internal address returned 401 without auth; authenticated Caddy→OmniRoute `/healthz` returned 200; the Docker host-port mapping `127.0.0.1:20129` is inactive on the internal topology. The extension still pins that loopback origin, so a stable usable endpoint is **NOT IMPLEMENTED**. Work lacks Docker-daemon permission; no dynamic IP/container identity was queried or made permanent. The candidate uses `--internal`, intended to block public egress, but host-gateway access is not excluded and actual provider destinations/allowlist are unknown; egress is **NOT VERIFIED**. No provider inference occurred. Existing Main decision `OMNIROUTE_DEFER / UNRESOLVED_SECURITY_BOUNDARY` still prevents inventing a network bridge or broad outbound access. `omniroute=deferred` and default `gpt-6-sol` remain unchanged. Exact blocker/next action: Main must specify a trusted stable endpoint plus a bounded, enforceable egress policy, or retain deferred/manual-only; no single safe host mutation can solve both with evidence currently available.

They are now active/running with MainPIDs 77893/77895/77894 and `NRestarts=0`; each has exactly one loopback listener matching its systemd MainPID: embeddings `127.0.0.1:1934`, OpenViking `127.0.0.1:1933`, Hindsight `127.0.0.1:8889`. All three health endpoints returned HTTP200 (`ok`, OpenViking 0.4.19, Hindsight healthy). There is no separate HTTP manager daemon: the existing manager is the stable shell lifecycle script plus the Pi Second Brain controller; `phi` `/memory-status` reported Second Brain OK, OpenViking/Hindsight available, ledger and ACE available.

Real stable-CLI RPC E2E used `/home/tasso/.local/bin/phi` in `--offline --no-session` mode: one Pi process ran `/memory remember` and exited0 with `Memory ACCEPTED ... local services=OK`; a second independent Pi process recovered the unique harmless datum through `sources=openviking`, then reported backend health OK. A separate fresh process ran the policy-supported historical query and recovered the same datum through `sources=hindsight`. Primary and Hindsight receipts are under `logs/second-brain-*.json` (mode 0600 where JSON); the preliminary route-miss/driver diagnostics are retained rather than hidden.

A normal TTY `/home/tasso/.local/bin/phi --offline` launch reached Pi 0.87.1, invoked the existing optional-startup path, and `/memory-status` showed both services available; the TTY client then exited via EOF. `config/second-brain.json` remains `auto`, and the existing manager performs status-then-start with bounded timeout/fail-soft behavior. User units remain `disabled` for systemd login/default-target autostart: this is not needed for the supported on-demand lifecycle, which prepares services when the user launches interactive `phi`. No unit files, stable target, Pi settings, auth, service credentials or code were modified. A cold-start-from-inactive through Phi was not simulated because the shared services were started and left healthy; existing `tests/phi-optional-startup.test.mjs` covers the stopped-manager→start branch. ## Optional-services release closure — OmniRoute manual-only (2026-10-28)

OmniRoute is **not verified usable through Phi** in this release. The candidate and promoted target both register the existing OpenAI-compatible provider (`extensions/omniroute.ts`, SHA-256 `c101c9c59e5b423a54e352a8966596fcc0113d6974f6ad0c0610888bb22fbf82`) and expose catalog entries, but `config/optional-services.json` is `{"version":1,"omniroute":"deferred"}` (SHA-256 `204bbee592ed549630c2ffb5ce5fea836961bc7d1ddeb1c7c815915ff5129b83`), so the existing coordinator returns `DEFERRED_FAIL_CLOSED` without launching it. A raw TCP provider URL is not accepted as a secure integration: the established Main boundary still lacks same-UID client authorization, reliable backend-process/listener attestation and effective egress policy. No bridge, proxy, service, provider call or OmniRoute process was started; local-fixture provider invocation was therefore not performed because it would test transport only, not satisfy the missing trust boundary. OmniRoute stays manual-only/deferred for this release; this is a bounded release disposition, not a request to reopen architecture.

The protected installation remains present at `/opt/phi/omniroute/3.8.50`; read-only inspection found root-owned `AppRun` mode 0755 and SHA-256 `74aefdd18cdc66837ef6ef55381d95a2dcd33b1149c2b3a003350f36761a7db7`. No OmniRoute listener on 20128 or matching AppRun process was observed in the bounded process/listener snapshot; no attempt was made to launch the app. The configured provider environment variable names were present in the Work process environment, but their values were not read or emitted. This is not evidence that Phi can call the gateway or that the endpoint is trusted.

SearXNG and Second Brain are unchanged; their latest recorded evidence remains the prior real `web_research` RPC PASS and Second Brain cross-session memory/service PASS in the preceding entries. Neither was retested in this closure. No product code, stable target, auth, routing, Docker object or service was changed. Local optional-services milestone is **CLOSED** with OmniRoute explicitly excluded from supported Phi use for this release. Current branch/HEAD remain `feature/phi-optional-services-startup` / `4c0031167f97838f977dd519397beea1b098264c`; the docs changes are local, and the pre-existing untracked `logs/` are retained, not staged.

## OmniRoute trust-boundary decision — existing sandbox insufficient for all same-UID processes (2026-10-25)

## 2026-10-04 — D6 ordinary-publication decision and post-revocation controller slice (LOCAL ONLY)

`D6-ORDINARY-WORKFLOW-PUBLICATION-001` is recorded in `harness/DECISIONS.md`. A strictly opt-in Worker reservation now permits the controller to revoke the child session/tool authority while retaining its private verified draft until controller publication or final disposal; the default reservation lifecycle is unchanged. Portable in-flight revocation regression passes, and a real native V5 post-revocation four-operation regression has been added but **not yet run on Windows**. This does **not** integrate or activate ordinary Windows `secure_coding_workflow`: its entry guard still rejects Windows before delegation, and no trusted ordinary-task publication intent is bound to the one-shot grant. No provider/model call was made.

Local focused `phi-worker-transport` **10 PASS / 0 FAIL / 7 native skips** and `phi-publication` **19 PASS / 0 FAIL / 30 native skips**. Full local `npm run check` **PASS** outside the managed process-spawn restriction: `npm test` **1014 total / 695 PASS / 0 FAIL / 319 native skips**, syntax, installed-Pi TypeScript and package validation PASS. The first restricted run failed to spawn `git` (`EPERM`) in unrelated fixture tests; it is not a product/test failure and no test or timeout was weakened. No new CI run/native Windows certificate.

**Mandatory Main boundary found before ordinary integration:** V5 native `ValidateHeader` accepts exactly four operations in fixed `REPLACE, CREATE, DELETE, RENAME` order, `RunMixed` physically executes all four, and the JS publisher rejects every other V5 shape. Standalone `CREATE` exists only as a no-clobber primitive (`NATIVE_CREATE_PRIMITIVE_NOT_PROJECT_TRANSACTION`), not a journaled publication transaction. ## Continuation — native process ownership candidate

Resumed from `d8a7834b861a231f450b53beb1d8a476eb4f7eac`, exact branch and existing native CI confirmed; protected/auth hashes unchanged. No foundation redesign or authentication/routing change.

Implemented a Windows x64 system-.NET helper from tracked C# source and a shared-interface dispatch for trusted non-interactive verification only. Native Job assignment occurs atomically through `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST` at process creation, before resume; only three explicit stdio handles are inherited, never the Job handle. Job limits are code-owned (32 processes, no breakaway); lifetime <=120s. Normal exit, timeout and failure terminate the owned Job; caller death waits on a generation-checked parent handle; abrupt helper death uses kill-on-last-close. Requests use a bounded stdin pipe, not request files or command-line payloads. Node bounds output/input and preserves failed exit/abort semantics. Build identity is local integrity evidence, NOT an ACL/signed-runtime attestation or sandbox certificate.
